## management-governance — module synthesis

_Source: `99_management-governance-synthesis.md`_

# Management-Governance Module — BURL (Synthesis)

## Abstract

Burlington’s management looks competent, but shareholder alignment is ordinary rather than owner-like. The team met or exceeded all three completed targets reviewed, while four-year capital spending and buybacks supported operating growth and reduced diluted shares, though strict net debt rose and repurchases were not tested against intrinsic value. Pay is tied mainly to adjusted EBIT and adjusted EPS, yet insiders own only 1.3%, logged no open-market buys, and were net sellers. The clearest governance concern is repeated adjusted-profit exclusions, including $21.8 million in FY2025, alongside a coverage-limited legal and people sweep. The resulting call is Standard / mixed: usable evidence, no gate failure, but not enough alignment or clean disclosure to call stewardship exemplary.

## 1. Stewardship Verdict

- **Verdict:** Standard / mixed
- **NON-NEGOTIABLE GATE: PASS** — no hard disqualifier, Critical module red flag, Disqualifying-graded controller/CEO/CFO/chair, or material undisclosed legal matter was evidenced. Coverage is 131/179 registry items answered.
- **Hard disqualifier flagged (business-model/01)?** N — business-model/01 reports “No disqualifier triggered.”
- Management quality /100: 83
- Capital allocation /100: 68
- Incentive alignment /100: 79
- Shareholder friendliness /100: 71
- Disclosure candor /100: 68
- People & network integrity /100: 65 (79 before the mandatory coverage cap)
- Audit & assurance quality /100: 72
- RPT & leakage risk /100 *(higher = worse)*: 35
- Contingent-liability risk /100 *(higher = worse)*: 20
- Accounting-forensics risk /100 *(higher = worse)*: 25
- Legal & regulatory risk /100 *(higher = worse)*: 40
- Governance risk /100 *(higher = worse)*: 40
- Data quality /100: 78
- Overall usefulness /100: 82
- **Checklist coverage:** 131/179 (73.2%) — 48 Green / 80 Amber / 3 Red / 48 N-A
- Insider ownership (one line): directors and executives beneficially own 1.3%; the CEO owns about 0.74%, 72.1% of his disclosed position is options or near-term RSUs, and the reviewed year had five open-market sales, no open-market buys, and net sales of 9,231 shares. [FY2026 DEF 14A, Security Ownership; Form 4 review through 2026-09-29]
- Biggest governance signal (one line): all three completed operating targets reviewed were met or exceeded, but the people directing capital have thin purchased equity and no observed open-market buying. [01_management-and-track-record, Sections 2–3; 04_ownership-and-insider-behavior, Sections 1–3]
- **Checklist Risk:** max(35 RPT, 20 contingent-liability, 25 accounting-forensics, 40 legal/regulatory) = **40**, bound by Legal & regulatory risk.
- **Governance Score /100:** 0.14×68 + 0.11×79 + 0.11×71 + 0.10×68 + 0.11×83 + 0.09×72 + 0.11×65 + 0.11×(100−40) + 0.12×(100−40) = 69.38, reported as **69**.
- **Confidence-Adjusted Governance Score /100:** 69 × 70 / 100 = **48** (48.3 before whole-number reporting).
- **Governance Rating:** Watchlist
- **Confidence Score /100:** 70 — capped by the coverage-limited legal and person/entity database sweeps.
- **Red-Flag Count / Critical Red-Flag Count:** 2 / 0

## 1A. Module Disconfirmation

- **Strongest bear point:** the incentive system uses absolute adjusted EBIT for the annual bonus, recurring adjusted-profit exclusions continued, and insiders recorded no open-market purchases; the FY2025 non-GAAP reconciliation excluded $21.8 million. [FY2026 DEF 14A, Compensation Discussion & Analysis; FY2025 Form 10-K, MD&A / non-GAAP reconciliation]
- **Strongest bull point:** management met or exceeded all three completed targets that the track-record specialist could test, while FY2025 CFO/PAT was 2.02× and CFO/EBITDA was 99.2%, so delivery is supported by cash rather than only adjusted profit. [01_management-and-track-record, Sections 2–3; earnings/06_earnings-quality, Sections 1–3]
- **Single killer risk:** debt-funded expansion and buybacks could be rewarded through adjusted EBIT and EPS even if per-share value weakens; this becomes thesis-killing stewardship evidence if leverage rises while repurchases fail to reduce diluted shares or cash returns deteriorate. [02_capital-allocation-scorecard, Sections 1–5; FY2026 DEF 14A, Compensation Discussion & Analysis]
- **Disconfirming evidence already visible:** a 91.7%-independent board, independent chair, clean current audit and ICFR opinions, $0 of FY2025 reportable Item 404 transactions, and no hard disqualifier argue against a negative integrity conclusion; historical auditor discipline and incomplete legal screens prevent calling the record clean. [FY2026 DEF 14A, Board and Certain Relationships; FY2025 Form 10-K, Auditor Report; business-model/01_disqualifier-scan]

| contingent-liabilities-and-commitments | 20/100 risk — low quantified risk, incomplete categories | $58.0m of letters of credit equals 2.90% of Q2 equity; $2,558.5m of inventory commitments is separate from contingent loss and lacks cancellation detail. |
| accounting-forensics | 25/100 risk — no measured manipulation pattern | Beneish M-score was −2.6207 and Dechow F-score 0.8819; DEPI and soft assets were Amber, while cash authenticity could not be tested on a matched basis. |
| regulatory-legal-and-compliance | 40/100 risk — hygiene lapses, watch | A $5.2m resolved wage settlement and an active unquantified contract case reconcile to disclosed categories; database coverage limits force the 40 risk floor. |

## 3. Reconciliation


- **Execution versus alignment:** management quality is 83 because the team delivered the three completed targets that could be tested, but capital allocation is 68, ownership alignment is 56, and insiders made no observed open-market buys. The conservative read is competent operators with conventional, not owner-like, alignment.
- **Board labels versus practical independence:** 91.7% of directors meet the legal independence test and the chair is independent, but five directors had tenure over ten years and shareholders cannot call a special meeting or act by written consent. The board is adequate, not exemplary.
- **Candor versus adjusted performance:** the tariff refund was quantified precisely, but repeated lease, impairment and litigation exclusions fired RF-DISC-002. The transparent single event does not erase the recurring adjusted-profit pattern.
- **Current audit quality versus gatekeeper history:** clean financial-statement and ICFR opinions and a 5.7% non-audit/audit fee ratio support the current assurance read; 43 years of tenure, historical PCAOB orders, and an unidentified signing partner keep A4-02 and A4-13 Red. No BURL-specific audit failure was established.
- **No reportable RPT versus group funding opacity:** the proxy reports $0 of FY2025 Item 404 transactions, while the 10-K shows a $297.1m joint note to BCFWC/BMC and 99.976% of consolidated cash below the Parent. These are disclosed intragroup facts with $409.0m of upstream contributions, not proof of minority leakage; entity-level cash and guarantee mapping remains missing.
- **Cash-supported earnings versus forensic gaps:** CFO/PAT of 2.02× and CFO/EBITDA of 99.2% support earnings, while DEPI, soft assets, flat cash taxes, and unmatched cash-interest data remain monitors. The measured battery is not Red.
- **Legal/person outcomes versus search coverage:** no person was attributed a Material or Disqualifying fact and the two public cases reconcile to filed legal categories, but the official regulator, court, sanctions, exchange, and rating interfaces were incomplete. “Nothing material found” is not a clearance.
- **Subsidiary count discrepancy:** 09 records 25 named subsidiaries, while 07’s scope-boundary prose refers to 26 current subsidiaries. No conclusion depends on the one-entity difference; obtain the legal-entity chart before using either count as a control threshold.

## 5. Stewardship Summary

Management has delivered the completed targets available for testing, and the cash record supports the operating result, but the evidence does not show an owner-operator culture. FY2022–FY2025 capital spending of $2,880.2 million absorbed 80.9% of CFO, while $1,094.8 million of buybacks reduced diluted shares 2.69%; strict net debt then rose from $849.8 million at FY2025 year-end to $1,209.9 million at Q2 FY2026, so future repurchases need a price-and-leverage test. Incentives point partly toward per-share value through adjusted-EPS PSUs, but an absolute adjusted-EBIT bonus, 35% time-based RSUs, 1.3% insider ownership, and no observed open-market buying weaken that link. Minority holders have one vote per share, an independent chair, and all-independent committees, but long director tenure and weak direct-action rights temper the protection. The best reason to trust this team is delivered execution backed by cash; the main reason not to grant higher confidence is that adjusted metrics, thin purchased ownership, and incomplete legal/person screens leave room for stewardship quality to look better than it is.

The riskiest decision-relevant subjects are the CEO, CFO, chair, and General Counsel only because their Tier-A identity/court/global screens are incomplete; none carries an attributed adverse fact and no grade trips the gate. The check reached filed current/former leaders, group lineage, marks, address cluster, and one founder, but it did not reach complete identifier-linked courts, sanctions/PEP, regulator, or entity-registry records.

## 6. What Would Change The Stewardship Verdict?

| Current Verdict | What Would Strengthen It | What Would Weaken It | Data Needed |
|---|---|---|---|
| Standard / mixed | Sustained target delivery with lower strict net debt; repurchases below a documented value threshold that continue to reduce diluted shares; open-market executive buying; removal of recurring adjusted exclusions; completed legal/person screens with no material findings. | Buybacks or expansion funded by rising leverage without per-share gains; repeated adjusted-cost exclusions; material case or person finding omitted from filings; failed CHRO/CEO succession; further board entrenchment or rising vote opposition. | Identifier-linked paid people/entity court, sanctions, PEP and regulator batch; formal 2026 annual-meeting voting results; legal-entity cash/debt/guarantee chart; FY2026 exclusion-to-cash reconciliation; current audit partner and inspection record. |

## 7. Note To The Final Synthesizer


- Stewardship is **Standard / mixed**: the strongest positive is three of three completed targets met or exceeded with cash-supported earnings; thin purchased ownership, adjusted-metric pay, and recurring exclusions prevent a higher call.
- **NON-NEGOTIABLE GATE: PASS** with 131/179 checklist items answered; none of the four gate conditions was evidenced.
- Capital allocation produced growth and a 2.69% diluted-share reduction, but $2,880.2m of four-year capex and $1,094.8m of buybacks must now be judged beside Q2 FY2026 strict net debt of $1,209.9m.
- Incentives partly target per-share value through adjusted-EPS PSUs, but annual bonus is absolute adjusted EBIT; insiders own 1.3%, made no observed open-market purchase, and were net sellers.
- **People read:** 40 Clean-on-own-record grades, all coverage-limited; no Minor, Material, or Disqualifying grades. The highest-priority unresolved issue is missing identifier-linked coverage, not an attributed adverse fact.
- **Checklist read:** 73.2% answered; Red items are A1-04, A4-02, and A4-13. Legal & regulatory risk at 40 is the binding Checklist Risk.
- Two unique High flags remain: RF-DISC-002 for recurring adjusted exclusions and RF-AUD-007 for historical audit-firm discipline. Critical count is zero.
- business-model/01 hard-disqualifier result, verbatim: **“No disqualifier triggered.”**
- No §24 turnaround, serial-acquirer, structurally unaligned-owner, or unresolved-integrity filter tripped; none imposed a conviction cap.
- The legal/person sweep coverage cap binds: People & network integrity 65, Legal risk 40, Confidence 70. Declared entity breadth overflow also caps People at 70 and Confidence at 80, but is not binding.
- Single highest-value next data request: an identifier-linked paid U.S. people-and-entity registry/court/sanctions/PEP/regulator batch for the 40-person roster, BURL’s former name, BCFWC, and BCF Direct.
- **This module supersedes business-model/11_capital-allocation-governance; use this synthesis verdict and scores as the primary governance read.**

## 8. Simple Summary


- Management met or beat every completed target the specialist could test.
- Capital spending and buybacks helped growth and reduced diluted shares, but debt rose; value creation is mixed, not proven.
- Pay rewards absolute adjusted EBIT and adjusted EPS, with 35% of long-term awards still time-based.
- Insiders own 1.3%, made no observed open-market buys, and were net sellers.
- Minority holders get one vote per share and an independent chair, but long board tenure and weak direct-action rights matter.
- Management quantified the tariff refund well, yet recurring adjusted exclusions weaken the candor read.
- No hard disqualifier or Critical red flag fired; the gate passes.
- The module is useful, but the master should cap confidence at 70 until legal and person/entity screens are complete.

## 6. Sufficiency Verdict

- **Verdict:** Sufficient
- **Reason:** The frozen pool has a current SEC proxy/compensation disclosure, beneficial-ownership and Form 4-sourced insider data, board and related-party disclosure, two annual filings plus multi-year financial history, an audit report and fee disclosure, and recent transcripts; all required core governance source types are present and extraction completed without failures.
- **Specialists that can run:** management track record; capital allocation; incentives; ownership; board; candor; people dossiers (with identifier and public-database work still required); audit quality; RPT/group forensics; contingent liabilities; accounting forensics; regulatory/legal (with public-database work still required).
- **Hard disqualifier already flagged by business-model/01_disqualifier-scan?** N — upstream scan says no disqualifier triggered.
- **Critical missing items:** formal AGM/annual-meeting voting-results filing; dedicated 8-K/exchange-compliance archive; primary rating-agency reports; and public legal/regulatory database sweep. These do not change the Sufficient verdict because the core proxy, ownership, board/RPT and multi-year capital-allocation requirements are met. The downstream owners must state coverage limits if their database sweep cannot run.
- **Single highest-value missing document:** formal 2026 annual-meeting voting-results filing (or the corresponding 8-K), because it is the missing direct evidence for minority dissent and resolution-level voting.

# Management & Track Record — BURL

## 1. Key Executives

| Role | Name | Appointed | Tenure | Relevant Background | Source |
|---|---|---:|---:|---|---|
| CEO | Michael O’Sullivan | September 2019 | 7.0 years | Professional manager, not a founder: Burlington CEO since 2019; formerly Ross Stores’ President and COO (2009–2019), after retail-strategy work at Bain & Company (1991–2003). | [S01: FY2026 Proxy Statement, p. 19] |
| CFO | Kristin Wolfe | August 2022 | 4.1 years | Former Ross Stores Group SVP, Corporate Finance; prior finance, strategy and store-operations roles at Ross from 2009. | [S01: FY2026 Proxy Statement, p. 20] |
| Group President & Chief Merchandising Officer | Jennifer Vecchio | July 2021 in current role; joined Burlington May 2015 | 5.2 years in current role; 11.4 years at Burlington | Former Burlington Chief Merchandising Officer and long-time Ross/Macy’s merchant. | [S01: FY2026 Proxy Statement, p. 20] |
| President & COO | Travis Marquette | October 2021 | 4.9 years | Former Ross CFO and earlier operations/finance leader; prior Bain, Carter’s and PwC roles. | [S01: FY2026 Proxy Statement, p. 20] |
| EVP & CHRO | Matthew Pasch | September 2023 | 3.0 years in current role; 16.4 years at Burlington | Internal HR leader since 2010. A July 2026 separation agreement set September 1, 2026 as his last day; the provided filing did not name a successor or a reason. | [S01: FY2026 Proxy Statement, p. 20] [S02: Q2 FY2026 Form 10-Q, Exhibit 10.1] |

O’Sullivan is a professional CEO, not a founder or owner-operator. The filing-supported officer group has deep off-price retail experience, particularly from Ross Stores, but the record does not establish a named CHRO successor after Pasch’s scheduled exit.

## 2. Management Stability

| Signal | Detail | Source |
|---|---|---|
| Senior departures (last 3 years) | One material officer transition is visible in the supplied filings: Pasch’s agreement, dated July 28, 2026, set September 1, 2026 as his last day as EVP/CHRO. The agreement does not disclose a successor or a cause. This is an Amber succession-continuity item, not evidence of misconduct. | [S02: Q2 FY2026 Form 10-Q, Exhibit 10.1] |
| CFO turnover | No CFO departure is visible in the FY2023–Q2 FY2026 materials. Wolfe has held the job since August 2022 and signed the Q2 FY2026 10-Q as principal financial officer on August 27, 2026. | [S01: FY2026 Proxy Statement, p. 20] [S02: Q2 FY2026 Form 10-Q, signature page] |
| CEO succession / continuity | O’Sullivan has led Burlington since September 2019. The supplied materials show continuity, but do not disclose a CEO succession candidate or timetable. | [S01: FY2026 Proxy Statement, p. 19] |

The CEO/CFO pair clears the role-tenure test. The 3.0-year CHRO tenure and planned exit mean the management layer is not churned, but its bench cannot be treated as fully demonstrated until Burlington identifies the replacement and transition plan.

## 3. Stated Strategy & Targets

Management’s strategy is to grow the store base while improving the off-price model: buy more merchandise in season, shift purchases between suppliers and categories as conditions change, and pursue a long-term 2,000-store opportunity. The 2,000-store objective has no stated completion date, so it is an aspiration rather than a target that can yet be scored. [S05: FY2025 Form 10-K, Item 1 — Our Off-Price Sourcing and Merchandising Model; Item 1 — Store Expansion and Real Estate Strategy]

For FY2026, after Q2 management guided to 10%–11% total-sales growth, 3%–4% comparable-store-sales growth, about 115 net new stores, 20–40 basis points of Adjusted EBIT-margin expansion, and Adjusted EPS of $11.77–$11.97. These are company-defined non-GAAP targets where stated; they remain untested as of September 29, 2026. [S04: Q2 FY2026 earnings release, Outlook, 2026-08-27]

## 4. Promise vs Delivery

| Prior Promise / Target | Period Set | Actual Outcome | Kept / Missed | Source |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| Adjusted EBIT target of $850m; threshold $723m and maximum $978m | FY2025 annual incentive plan, approved February 2025 | Adjusted EBIT was $923m. That is $73m, or 8.6%, above target and produced a 157% annual-incentive payout. The reported calculation excluded about $35m of bankruptcy-acquired-lease expense. | Kept — above target, below maximum. | [S01: FY2026 Proxy Statement, pp. 53–54] |
| Adjusted EPS growth target of 13.3% a year, with 20.0% maximum | Fiscal 2023 PSU awards; performance years FY2023–FY2025 | Adjusted EPS growth was 44% in FY2023, 35% in FY2024 and 20% in FY2025; each year earned the 200% maximum payout. | Kept — met or exceeded the stated maximum in all three reported years. | [S01: FY2026 Proxy Statement, pp. 55–56] |
| Q2 FY2026 sales +10%–12%, comparable-store sales +1%–3%, Adjusted EBIT margin +30–60bp and Adjusted EPS $2.05–$2.20 | Q1 FY2026 earnings release, 2026-05-28 | Q2 sales grew 11% and comparable-store sales 2%, both within range. Excluding tariff refunds, Adjusted EBIT margin grew 100bp and Adjusted EPS was $2.37, above the guided ranges. | Kept — sales and comparable sales in range; Adjusted margin and EPS above range. | [S03: Q1 FY2026 earnings release, Outlook, 2026-05-28] [S04: Q2 FY2026 earnings release, Operating Results, 2026-08-27] |
| FY2026 sales, comparable-sales, margin, EPS and 115-store targets | Q2 FY2026 earnings release, 2026-08-27 | Fiscal year ends January 30, 2027; no full-year outcome is available. | Not yet assessable. | [S04: Q2 FY2026 earnings release, Outlook, 2026-08-27] |

The three completed tests are company-defined Adjusted EBIT/EPS or quarterly guidance tests, not independent measures of economic value. They nevertheless show targets were published before the measured period and were met or exceeded. The current FY2026 outlook must remain a forecast, not a delivered result.

## 4A. Turnaround & Integrity Tests (CLAUDE.md §24, Filters 1 & 2)

| Test | Finding | Source |
|---|---|---|
| Is the thesis a turnaround (struggling business + new management / new plan)? | No. The current CEO has led since 2019 and the supplied strategy is continuation of an established store-rollout and off-price model, not a new-management rescue plan. | [S01: FY2026 Proxy Statement, p. 19] [S05: FY2025 Form 10-K, Item 1 — Store Expansion and Real Estate Strategy] |
| If yes: years of *proven* operating inflection actually delivered (not promised) | Not applicable. The FY2023–FY2025 Adjusted EPS delivery history is evidence of results, but it is not presented as proof of a turnaround. | [S01: FY2026 Proxy Statement, pp. 55–56] |
| Résumé / plan / consultant deck cited as the reason to believe? (not evidence) | No. The management read relies on dated delivery tests and filing-backed continuity, not on résumés alone. | [S01: FY2026 Proxy Statement, pp. 19–20, 53–56] |
| Routed integrity “buzz” from `business-model/01_disqualifier-scan` (unverified adverse signal) | No unverified integrity signal was routed in the available disqualifier scan. This is not an independent integrity clearance; person-level checks remain owned by agent 07. | [S06: business-model/01_disqualifier-scan, §§2–3, 2026-09-29] |

## 5. Management Read

The CEO/CFO core has been stable for roughly seven and four years respectively, and the completed scorecard has three dated targets that cleared. That supports a strong management-quality read, not an owner-operator conclusion. The biggest open management issue is execution of the CHRO transition: the evidence records Pasch’s exit but no named successor or succession process.

## management-governance / 02_capital-allocation-scorecard.md

_Source: `02_capital-allocation-scorecard.md`_

# Capital-Allocation Scorecard — BURL

Burlington Stores, Inc. is a U.S. GAAP issuer reporting in USD with a January fiscal year-end. This scorecard covers FY2022–FY2025 (fiscal years ended January 28, 2023 through January 31, 2026), with H1 FY2026 shown separately where it updates the assessment. The cross-module quick read and historical-financials report were available and used; upstream `01_management-and-track-record.md` was also available. No multi-year-data cap applies.

## 1. Uses of Capital (3–5 years)

| Use | Cumulative Amount | Currency | % of CFO (or FCF) | Source |
|---|---:|---|---:|---|
| Acquisitions (net) | 0.0 | USD millions | 0.0% of $3,559.9m CFO | No cash acquisitions in the FY2022–FY2025 Capital IQ cash-flow series; no material business acquisition identified in the FY2025 filing. [CIQ Financials Annual—Cash Flow, FY2022–FY2025; FY2025 Form 10-K, Item 7—Capital Expenditures, p.33] |
| Buybacks | 1,094.8 | USD millions | 30.8% of CFO | `Repurchase of Common Stock` cash-flow line; this includes program repurchases and may include withholding-related treasury purchases, so it is not identical to the announced-program spend. [CIQ Financials Annual—Cash Flow, FY2022–FY2025] |
| Dividends | 0.0 | USD millions | 0.0% of CFO | No dividends paid in the annual cash-flow series. [CIQ Financials Annual—Cash Flow, FY2022–FY2025] |
| Capex | 2,880.2 | USD millions | 80.9% of CFO | Cash paid for property and equipment. [FY2024 Form 10-K, Consolidated Statements of Cash Flows, pp.52–53; FY2025 Form 10-K, Consolidated Statements of Cash Flows, p.46] |
| Debt repayment | 1,109.0 | USD millions | 31.2% of CFO | Gross long-term debt repayments; debt issuance was also $1,547.9m over the same period. [CIQ Financials Annual—Cash Flow, FY2022–FY2025] |

Reported cumulative CFO was $3,559.9m and CFO less cash capex (FCF, cash left after total property investment) was $679.7m across FY2022–FY2025. The listed uses therefore cannot be read as a ranking of FCF alone: capex, repurchases and debt repayment collectively exceeded CFO and were partly funded by debt issuance and cash already on hand. [FY2024 Form 10-K, pp.52–53; FY2025 Form 10-K, p.46; CIQ Financials Annual—Cash Flow, FY2022–FY2025]

## 2. M&A Scorecard

No material M&A in the period. The annual cash-flow series shows no cash-acquisition line in FY2022–FY2025, and the FY2025 filing describes the large investment program as stores, supply chain, IT and a California distribution-centre purchase rather than a business acquisition. This is evidence against a serial-acquirer pattern, not proof that every immaterial asset purchase was absent. [CIQ Financials Annual—Cash Flow, FY2022–FY2025; FY2025 Form 10-K, Item 7—Capital Expenditures, p.33]

| Deal | Year | Price | Disclosed Rationale / Synergy | Delivered? | Opportunity Cost (divestitures forced / focus lost / options foregone) | Source |
|---|---|---:|---|---|---|---|
| No material business acquisition identified | FY2022–FY2025 | 0.0 cash acquisitions reported | Not applicable | Not applicable | No deal-linked divestiture, acquisition synergies, or debt-funded empire-building program was identified. The relevant opportunity cost is instead the $2,880.2m organic capex commitment, whose store- and supply-chain-level returns are not disclosed. | [CIQ Financials Annual—Cash Flow, FY2022–FY2025; FY2025 Form 10-K, Item 7—Capital Expenditures, p.33] |

**Serial-acquirer + opportunity-cost test (CLAUDE.md §24, Filter 4).** No serial-acquirer pattern is proven from the available data, so RF-CAP-004 and its score cap do not apply. The absence of acquisition spending does not itself prove value creation; it removes one common source of value destruction. The principal allocation question is whether the organic investment produces cash and per-share earnings after the expansion cycle.

## 3. Buyback Scorecard

| Year | Amount Spent | Currency | Shares Bought | Implied Avg Price | Share-Count Change | Offset SBC Dilution? | Price-Discipline Evidence | Judgment | Source |
|---|---:|---|---:|---:|---:|---|---|---|---|
| FY2022 | 316.9 | USD millions | Not disclosed for program purchases | Not determinable | 66.49m to 64.97m year-end shares (−1.52m) | No on net share-count evidence | No program-price disclosure located | Net share count fell, but price discipline cannot be assessed. | [CIQ Financials Annual—Cash Flow and Balance Sheet, FY2022] |
| FY2023 | 243.2 | USD millions | Not disclosed for program purchases | Not determinable | 64.97m to 63.92m (−1.05m) | No on net share-count evidence | No program-price disclosure located | Net share count fell, but price discipline cannot be assessed. | [CIQ Financials Annual—Cash Flow and Balance Sheet, FY2023] |
| FY2024 | 256.3 | USD millions | Not disclosed for program purchases | Not determinable | 63.92m to 63.20m (−0.71m) | No on net share-count evidence | No program-price disclosure located | Net share count fell, but price discipline cannot be assessed. | [CIQ Financials Annual—Cash Flow and Balance Sheet, FY2024] |
| FY2025 | 278.4 cash-flow total; 251.4 announced program | USD millions | 985,594 program shares | $255.1 program spend ÷ shares | 63.20m to 62.69m (−0.52m) | No on net share-count evidence | Announced repurchase amount and shares disclosed; no valuation threshold or price-range analysis disclosed | The program reduced share count; intrinsic-value discipline is not assessable from this module. | [FY2025 Form 10-K, Item 7—Share Repurchase Program, p.33; CIQ Financials Annual—Cash Flow and Balance Sheet, FY2025] |
| H1 FY2026 | 167.4 announced program | USD millions | 528,185 program shares | $316.9 program spend ÷ shares | 62.69m to 62.82m (+0.13m) | Not determinable: a convertible-note exchange issued 150,831 shares and other issuance may matter | No valuation threshold or price-range analysis disclosed | The program buyback did not translate into a net first-half reduction; the documented note-exchange issuance is a material offset. | [Q2 FY2026 Form 10-Q, Item 2—Share Repurchase Program, p.30; Note 4—Long-Term Debt, p.13; cover page] |

The FY2022–FY2025 cash-flow series shows $1,094.8m of gross common-stock repurchases against $96.4m of common-stock issuance, while fully diluted weighted-average shares fell from 65.901m in FY2022 to 64.126m in FY2025 (−2.69%). That supports a net per-share benefit over the completed four-year period, even though stock-based compensation expense totaled $345.7m. It does not establish that every dollar was spent below defensible value. [CIQ Financials Annual—Cash Flow and Income Statement, FY2022–FY2025]

The announced-program average rose from $255.1 in FY2025 to $316.9 in H1 FY2026. That difference is a fact, not a valuation verdict: program timing within each period and a formal valuation threshold are not disclosed. Intrinsic-value discipline not assessable from this module.

## 4. Dividends & Organic Reinvestment

| Metric | Value | Source |
|---|---|---|
| Payout ratio | 0.0%: no cash dividend paid in FY2022–FY2025 | [CIQ Financials Annual—Cash Flow, FY2022–FY2025] |
| Dividend coverage (FCF) | Not applicable: $0 dividend against cumulative $679.7m CFO-minus-capex FCF | [FY2024 Form 10-K, pp.52–53; FY2025 Form 10-K, p.46] |
| Documented dividend policy exists? Followed for ≥4 years? (A10-01) | Retention policy is stated: Burlington says it intends to retain available funds and future earnings for capex, business initiatives and potential capital-structure initiatives; it does not anticipate a near-term cash dividend. A formal multi-year dividend-payout policy and four years of payments under one are not disclosed. | [Q2 FY2026 Form 10-Q, Item 2—Dividends, p.30] |
| Incremental ROIC (Δ NOPAT / Δ invested capital) | Approximately 27.9% on a narrow, calculated strict-debt-plus-equity-less-cash basis: NOPAT rose from about $300.6m in FY2022 to $646.2m in FY2025 (+$345.6m); invested capital rose from about $1,398.0m to $2,637.4m (+$1,239.4m). This excludes operating-lease liabilities and is not a company-disclosed project-return measure. CIQ’s differently defined reported return-on-capital series rose from 4.68% to 7.42%, so neither metric proves store- or distribution-centre returns. | [CIQ Financials Annual—Income Statement, Balance Sheet and Ratios, FY2022 and FY2025] |

The two return measures point in the same directional direction but are not interchangeable. The narrow incremental calculation is high because it excludes operating leases and uses balance-sheet financing changes; CIQ’s return-on-capital series remains only 7.42% in FY2025 on its own definition. The conservative conclusion is that organic reinvestment has improved reported returns but is not yet proven at the project level. [CIQ Financials Annual—Income Statement, Balance Sheet and Ratios, FY2022 and FY2025]

## 4A. Distribution Conduct (A10-05)

| Test | Finding | Source |
|---|---|---|
| Mechanism rationale stated (dividend vs buyback, who benefits)? | Management states that retained funds support capex, business initiatives and potential capital-structure initiatives. It authorizes open-market or privately negotiated buybacks based on market and corporate/regulatory considerations, but does not disclose a valuation threshold or a quantitative dividend-versus-buyback decision rule. | [Q2 FY2026 Form 10-Q, Item 2—Share Repurchase Program and Dividends, p.30] |
| Promoter participation in any buyback (tender/open-market) | Not applicable to a promoter-tender test: BURL is a U.S. issuer with no promoter class. No tender offer or insider-specific buyback benefit is identified in the supplied materials. | [BURL 2026 DEF 14A, Ownership of Securities, pp.42–43; Q2 FY2026 Form 10-Q, Item 2—Share Repurchase Program, p.30] |
| Buyback timing vs promoter pledge / stake changes | Not applicable to promoter pledge; directors and corporate personnel are prohibited from pledging or margining company securities. The policy is not independent proof of compliance. | [BURL 2026 DEF 14A, Prohibition on Hedging and Pledging of Company Stock, pp.57–58] |

## 5. Capital-Allocation Read

**Mixed.** The best completed use is the four-year buyback program: cash repurchases exceeded stock issuance and the fully diluted weighted-average share count fell 2.69%, although H1 FY2026’s program was offset at the outstanding-share level by the note exchange and other issuance. The worst and most consequential use is the $2.88bn capex program: it consumed 80.9% of four-year CFO while reported FCF was only $679.7m, and Burlington does not disclose maintenance-versus-growth capex or project-level returns. No material M&A or debt-funded serial-acquirer pattern is visible; the single biggest capital-allocation signal is therefore whether new stores and supply-chain capacity convert this cash spend into durable per-share returns.

## Capital Allocation Score

| Component | Score | Max Score | Evidence |
|---|---:|---:|---|
| Organic reinvestment returns | 15 | 25 | Capex consumed 80.9% of four-year CFO. Return metrics improved but maintenance/growth and project-level returns are not disclosed. [FY2024 Form 10-K, pp.52–53; FY2025 Form 10-K, p.46; CIQ Financials Annual—Ratios] |
| M&A discipline | 18 | 20 | No cash acquisitions or serial-acquirer pattern identified; absence of M&A is not proof of organic returns. [CIQ Financials Annual—Cash Flow] |
| Buyback discipline | 14 | 20 | Net share count fell through FY2025 and program average prices are calculable for FY2025/H1 FY2026; intrinsic-value threshold not disclosed. [FY2025 Form 10-K, p.33; Q2 FY2026 Form 10-Q, p.30] |
| Dividend sustainability | 10 | 15 | No dividend, hence no debt-funded payout; retention policy is explained but not a formal multi-year payout policy. [Q2 FY2026 Form 10-Q, p.30] |
| Balance-sheet prudence | 5 | 10 | Gross debt repayments were exceeded by issuance, and strict net debt rose from $849.8m at FY2025-end to $1,209.9m at Q2 FY2026. [CIQ Financials Annual—Cash Flow; Q2 FY2026 Form 10-Q, p.5] |
| Per-share value creation | 6 | 10 | Fully diluted weighted-average shares declined 2.69% across FY2022–FY2025; H1 FY2026 outstanding shares rose after the note exchange. [CIQ Financials Annual—Income Statement; Q2 FY2026 Form 10-Q, cover; Note 4, p.13] |
| **Total** | **68** | **100** | **Mixed: the four-year per-share and no-M&A evidence is positive, but the heavy organic investment and rising latest strict net debt remain the test.** |

## management-governance / 03_incentives-and-compensation.md

_Source: `03_incentives-and-compensation.md`_

# Incentives & Compensation — BURL

## 1. Compensation Structure

Reporting currency: USD. Period: fiscal 2025, ended January 31, 2026. Amounts below are reported compensation; equity is grant-date fair value, not cash realized.

| Executive | Base | Annual Bonus | Long-Term Incentive | Cash vs Equity Mix | Source |
|---|---:|---:|---:|---|---|
| Michael O'Sullivan, CEO | $1.520m salary | $4.771m annual-incentive payout | $10.843m stock awards: $7.048m PSUs and $3.795m RSUs | $6.307m cash / $10.843m equity = 36.8% / 63.2% of $17.150m total | [S1: FY2026 Proxy Statement, pp. 53–55, 61–62] |
| Kristin Wolfe, CFO | $0.882m salary | $1.385m annual-incentive payout | $3.286m stock awards: $2.136m PSUs and $1.150m RSUs | $2.282m cash / $3.286m equity = 41.0% / 59.0% of $5.568m total | [S1: FY2026 Proxy Statement, pp. 53–55, 61–62] |

The annual bonus and stock awards made up 91.1% of the CEO's reported fiscal-2025 compensation and 83.9% of the CFO's; these percentages use the Summary Compensation Table's grant-date equity values and are not realized-pay measures. [S1: FY2026 Proxy Statement, p. 61]

## 2. Performance Metrics

| Plan | Metric | Weight | Per-Share/Returns or Size-Based? | Source |
|---|---|---:|---|---|
| Fiscal 2025 annual incentive | Adjusted EBIT; $850m target, $923m actual and 157% payout. The committee excluded about $35m of bankruptcy-acquired-lease expense in measuring the result. | 100% of annual-incentive formula | **Size-based / absolute-profit metric.** It rewards operating profit in dollars, not EBIT per share, ROIC, FCF per share, or TSR. | [S1: FY2026 Proxy Statement, pp. 53–54] |
| Fiscal 2025 LTIP — PSUs | Adjusted EPS growth over fiscal 2025–2027, assessed annually and paid after the three-year period based on the average result. | 65% of annual LTIP grant value | **Per-share.** Adjusted EPS growth is per-share earnings growth, but remains an adjusted measure. | [S1: FY2026 Proxy Statement, pp. 54–55] |
| Fiscal 2025 LTIP — RSUs | Continued employment; 25% vests on each of four anniversaries. | 35% of annual LTIP grant value | **Neither a returns nor size metric.** It is time-based equity whose realized value moves with BURL's share price. | [S1: FY2026 Proxy Statement, pp. 54–55, 62] |
| Fiscal 2023 PSU cycle, paid after fiscal 2025 | Adjusted EPS growth; 13.3% target and 20.0% maximum each year. Actual growth was 44%, 35%, and 20% in fiscal 2023–2025, producing a 200% payout. | 100% of that PSU award | **Per-share**, subject to the plan's adjusted-EPS definition. | [S1: FY2026 Proxy Statement, pp. 47, 55–56] |

The metric mix is not a pure per-share/returns design. The long-term performance award uses per-share Adjusted EPS, but the entire annual cash plan uses absolute Adjusted EBIT and the disclosure does not put a return-on-capital or free-cash-flow-per-share measure into either plan. [S1: FY2026 Proxy Statement, pp. 53–55]

## 3. Pay vs Performance

| Signal | Detail | Source |
|---|---|---|
| CEO total pay (latest) | $17.150m in fiscal 2025, up 11.4% from $15.401m in fiscal 2024. This is reported total compensation, including grant-date equity fair value. | [S1: FY2026 Proxy Statement, pp. 61, 70] |
| Pay vs peers (if available) | The independent consultant Meridian and a compensation peer group informed design, but the supplied proxy does not disclose a CEO/CFO peer-median pay figure or percentile. Relative pay magnitude is therefore not assessable from the admitted evidence. | [S1: FY2026 Proxy Statement, pp. 45, 54] |
| Pay-for-performance alignment | CEO reported total compensation rose at a 10.2% CAGR from fiscal 2022 to fiscal 2025, versus GAAP net income growth of 38.4% over the same interval. That comparison is supportive but not a realized-pay or peer analysis. The fiscal-2025 annual bonus paid 157% after Adjusted EBIT reached $923m versus $850m target. | [S1: FY2026 Proxy Statement, pp. 53–54, 61, 70] |
| Say-on-pay support (%) | Approximately 89% of votes cast supported the 2025 advisory say-on-pay vote; the roughly 11% opposition is below the 20% significant-dissent threshold in the checklist. | [S1: FY2026 Proxy Statement, pp. 40, 49] |

## 3A. Statutory & Code Pay Gates (A6-01, A6-02, A6-05, A6-06, A6-09)

| Test | Raw value | Band applied | Verdict | Source |
|---|---:|---|---|---|
| Aggregate managerial pay vs the statutory cap (India: Sec 197 — 11% aggregate; ≤5% single MD/WTD, ≤10% all executives, NEDs ≤1%/3%; loss years per Schedule V) | Not applicable — BURL is a U.S. SEC issuer and the proxy contains no Indian Sec. 197 cap. | Apply local regime; no U.S. statutory percentage cap identified in the supplied proxy. | Not Applicable | [S1: FY2026 Proxy Statement, cover; Executive Compensation, pp. 45–73] |
| Promoter-executive pay vs the extra gates (India: LODR Reg 17(6)(e) — ₹5cr or 2.5% of net profit individually, 5% aggregate) | Not applicable — no promoter group or SEBI LODR regime. Quantitative CEO/CFO peer-pay benchmark not disclosed. | U.S.-issuer local-equivalent assessment; do not apply Indian promoter gates. | Insufficient Data for peer comparison | [S1: FY2026 Proxy Statement, pp. 42, 45, 54] |
| MD/CEO pay growth vs 3-year PAT CAGR | CEO reported total-compensation CAGR: 10.2% (FY2022–FY2025); GAAP net-income CAGR: 38.4% over the same three fiscal years. | Green when pay does not outrun profit; this is a local-equivalent comparison, not an Indian PAT test. | Green | [S1: FY2026 Proxy Statement, pp. 61, 70] |
| CEO-to-median ratio + trend of the MEDIAN (rising exec pay on a falling median is the flag) | CEO $17,149,564; median associate $10,827; ratio 1,584:1. The median associate was a part-time store associate. | Red requires an unjustified ratio >200× or rising executive pay while median pay falls. The supplied evidence gives no prior median figure or sector ratio. | Amber | [S1: FY2026 Proxy Statement, p. 69] |
| Severance / change-of-control terms (double-trigger only; ≤2 years' pay) | CIC plan pays 2.5× base salary plus target bonus for CEO and 2.0× for other participants, only after a qualifying termination within two years of a change in control; equity has no automatic single-trigger vesting. | Green: double trigger and ≤2 years' pay; Red: >3× or single trigger. CEO multiple is between the bands. | Amber | [S1: FY2026 Proxy Statement, pp. 6, 66–68] |
| Clawback / malus policy (and: was any trigger acted on?) | Section 954 policy requires recovery of erroneously awarded incentive pay during the three years before an accounting restatement and without regard to culpability; additional policy covers financial harm from willful action or gross negligence. | Green: restatement/misconduct policy with a lookback; Red: none or recovery not pursued after a restatement. No recovery event is disclosed in the supplied evidence. | Green | [S1: FY2026 Proxy Statement, pp. 57, 60] |
| Problematic practices (repricing without a vote, gross-ups, single-trigger vesting, guaranteed multi-year bonuses) | Proxy lists no excise-tax gross-ups, no automatic single-trigger equity vesting, no guaranteed bonuses/salary increases, no reload options, and fixed annual grant timing. | Green: none of the listed practices; Red: any listed practice. | Green | [S1: FY2026 Proxy Statement, pp. 6, 58, 67] |
| Dissent response (any ≥20% vote against pay met with a disclosed response ≤6 months?) | 89% say-on-pay support; approximately 11% against. | Significant dissent norm: ≥20% against; below that level no remedial response is triggered. | Green | [S1: FY2026 Proxy Statement, pp. 40, 49] |

## 3B. Human Capital (A12-01 … A12-04)

| Test | Raw value | Band applied | Verdict | Source |
|---|---:|---|---|---|
| Attrition vs industry median | No BURL attrition rate or industry-median comparison disclosed. Available evidence cannot establish either outcome. | Insufficient Data | [S1: FY2026 Proxy Statement, pp. 26, 31] [S2: FY2025 Form 10-K, Item 1 — Human Capital Resources, pp. 4–5] |

## 4. Incentive Alignment Read

**Mixed.** Long-term pay has a real per-share element: 65% of the fiscal-2025 LTIP is in three-year Adjusted-EPS PSUs, while 35% is time-vesting equity. But annual cash pay is 100% tied to absolute Adjusted EBIT, and the fiscal-2025 calculation excluded $35m of lease-related expense; there is no ROIC or FCF-per-share measure. The biggest signal is therefore a real but incomplete shift toward per-share rewards, rather than a pay design focused only on per-share value creation. [S1: FY2026 Proxy Statement, pp. 53–55]

# Ownership & Insider Behavior — BURL

## 1. Ownership

| Holder | % Held | Share Class | Bought or Granted? | Source |
|---|---:|---|---|---|
| CEO Michael O’Sullivan | 0.74% beneficial ownership (466,285 shares) | Common stock | The proxy includes 322,218 exercisable options and 14,157 RSUs vesting within 60 days (72.1% of the disclosed beneficial total). It does not identify the acquisition basis of the remaining 129,910 shares; an open-market purchase cannot be inferred. | [BURL 2026 DEF 14A, Ownership of Securities, pp. 42–43] |
| Other directors and executive officers, aggregate | 0.61% incremental; 1.3% including CEO (852,036 shares across 16 people) | Common stock | A material part of the disclosed group total is equity compensation and options. The proxy does not provide a group-level bought-versus-granted split. | [BURL 2026 DEF 14A, Ownership of Securities, pp. 42–43] |
| Largest disclosed institutional holders | Capital International Investors 13.9%; JPMorgan 10.8%; BlackRock 10.3%; The Vanguard Group 10.2% | Common stock | Institutional holdings, not insider ownership. The proxy’s underlying 13G dates range from 2024 to 2025 and say holdings may have changed; the newer CIQ history is used below for the trend. | [BURL 2026 DEF 14A, Ownership of Securities, pp. 42–43] |
| Promoter / controlling bloc | Not applicable — no promoter or controlling-owner bloc is disclosed | One common-stock class | Not applicable. The officers-and-directors group owns 1.3%, and the proxy names four separate 10%+ institutional holders rather than a controller. | [BURL 2026 DEF 14A, Ownership of Securities, pp. 42–43] |

Total insider ownership is 1.3% on the proxy’s beneficial-ownership basis as of March 25, 2026. That is ownership exposure, but not an owner-operator stake. The table includes options and near-term RSUs, so it must not be presented as cash bought in the market.

## 2. Insider Transactions (last 12 months)

| Insider | Buy / Sell | Amount | Date | Notes (10b5-1?) | Source |
|---|---|---:|---|---|---|
| Kristin Wolfe, CFO | Open-market sale | 2,932 shares; $1.00m | 2026-06-17 | No 10b5-1 designation is shown in the frozen vendor export. Separate option exercises and sales occurred on the same date. | [Capital IQ Public Ownership → Insider Trading, Form 4 filed 2026-06-22] |
| Stephen Ferroni, Chief Accounting Officer | Open-market sale | 1,311 shares; $0.44m | 2026-06-11 | No 10b5-1 designation shown; the filing also records an exercise-and-sale transaction. | [Capital IQ Public Ownership → Insider Trading, Form 4 filed 2026-06-15] |
| Matthew Pasch, then EVP/CHRO | Open-market sale | 3,773 shares; $1.20m | 2026-06-05 | No 10b5-1 designation shown. He later left the company on September 1, 2026; the vendor export does not state why the sale occurred. | [Capital IQ Public Ownership → Insider Trading, Form 4 filed 2026-06-09] |
| Travis Marquette, President & COO | Open-market sale | 894 shares; $0.28m | 2026-05-04 | No 10b5-1 designation shown. | [Capital IQ Public Ownership → Insider Trading, Form 4 filed 2026-05-05] |
| Michael Goodwin, independent director | Open-market sale | 321 shares; $0.10m | 2026-03-24 | No 10b5-1 designation shown. | [Capital IQ Public Ownership → Insider Trading, Form 4 filed 2026-03-26] |
| Michael O’Sullivan, CEO | Sale to issuer / withholding | 6,423 shares; $2.03m | 2026-05-01 to 2026-05-04 | Not an open-market sale. The same filing records 12,292 RSUs as an other acquisition. | [Capital IQ Public Ownership → Insider Trading, Form 4 filed 2026-05-05] |
| Jennifer Vecchio, Group President & CMO | Option exercises and sale | 20,920 shares disposed; $3.49m shown for the combined exercise-and-sale record | 2026-06-12 | Not an open-market purchase. The record includes 20,920 exercised shares and contemporaneous sales. | [Capital IQ Public Ownership → Insider Trading, Form 4 filed 2026-06-16] |

Net insider activity: **net selling on the open-market measure** — five sales, zero purchases, and net **−9,231 shares** through the latest open-market event on June 22, 2026. The all-transaction CIQ measure is net **+34,926 shares** through August 5, 2026, but it includes grants, exercises, issuer/tax-withholding transactions and other non-market legs; it is not evidence of net cash buying. [CIQ Public Ownership → Insider Trading, `insider_open_market` and `insider_net_activity` facts, through 2026-06-22 and 2026-08-05]

The May 4 Marquette open-market sale preceded the May 28 Q1 release, but that release reported 14% sales growth, 6% comparable-store growth and adjusted EPS of $2.10, above guidance. The available evidence therefore does not establish insider selling ahead of weak results. [BURL Q1 FY2026 earnings release, 2026-05-28]

## 3. Pledging & Encumbrance

| Insider | Shares Pledged | % of Their Holding | Source |
|---|---:|---:|---|
| Directors and corporate personnel | No pledging disclosed in the reviewed proxy materials | Not computable from a pledge schedule | [BURL 2026 DEF 14A, Prohibition on Hedging and Pledging of Company Stock, pp. 57–58] |

No pledging disclosed. The proxy says directors and corporate personnel may not hold BURL securities in a margin account or pledge them as loan collateral. That prohibition is a control, not independent proof of every individual’s compliance; no pool filing reports a pledge. The current-run disqualifier scan likewise finds 0 disclosed pledged shares out of the 852,036-share officer-and-director group and did not trigger its >50% pledge disqualifier. [S02: BURL 2026 DEF 14A, Prohibition on Hedging and Pledging of Company Stock, pp. 57–58; S12: business-model/01_disqualifier-scan, §1]

## 4. Control Structure

| Feature | Detail | Source |
|---|---|---|
| Dual-class / super-voting | No. Each outstanding common share carries one vote; the proxy highlights no unequal voting rights and no cumulative voting. | [BURL 2026 DEF 14A, Governance Highlights, p. 3; About the Annual Meeting, p. 7] |
| Controlled-company status | Not disclosed. The directors-and-officers group owns 1.3%, while four separate institutions are each disclosed above 10%; this is dispersed ownership rather than a controlled-company structure. | [BURL 2026 DEF 14A, Ownership of Securities, pp. 42–43] |
| Shareholder bloc with board rights | No special bloc, veto right or board-nomination right is disclosed. The bylaws allow a holder or group of up to 20 holders with at least 3% continuous ownership for three years to nominate up to the greater of two nominees or 20% of the board through proxy access. | [BURL 2026 DEF 14A, Proxy Access, p. 22] |
| Controlling-owner objective alignment *(Filter 6)* | No government controller, listed-parent subsidiary, or sprawling controller-owned conglomerate is disclosed. Filter 6 does not trip. | [BURL 2026 DEF 14A, Ownership of Securities, pp. 42–43] |

The ownership structure gives outside shareholders voting rights on the same one-share/one-vote basis as insiders. No controller-related value-trap cap or RF-OWN-004 is warranted from the available filings.

## 4B. Ownership-Structure Hygiene (A3-08, A3-09, A3-10)

| Test | Finding | Source |
|---|---|---|
| Promoter-group classification (any reclassification to "public"? persons-acting-in-concert complete?) | Not applicable. BURL is a U.S. issuer with no promoter-group regime in the reviewed disclosure. | [BURL 2026 DEF 14A, Ownership of Securities, pp. 42–43] |
| Beneficial-ownership chain (ultimate owners identifiable? trusts/offshore layers?) | The proxy identifies each holder above 5% and cites the underlying Schedule 13G/A filings. The underlying dates are stale for several holders, so this is transparency of disclosed identity, not a current ownership certification. | [BURL 2026 DEF 14A, Ownership of Securities, pp. 42–43] |
| Takeover-code disclosure hygiene (India: SAST Reg 29 timeliness, creeping-acquisition limits, promoter ≤75%) | Not applicable to SAST. The supplied proxy references Schedule 13G/A filings but the pool does not contain a complete SEC beneficial-ownership filing history, so filing timeliness cannot be tested. | [BURL 2026 DEF 14A, Ownership of Securities, pp. 42–43] |

## 5. Blunt Read

Management has 1.3% beneficial ownership, but the disclosed total includes options and RSUs, and no open-market purchases appear in the last 12 months. The cleaner behavior signal is five open-market sales totaling 9,231 shares; it is modest relative to 62.8m shares outstanding and is not evidenced as sales ahead of weak results. The main positive is control fairness: no controller, one vote per share, and no disclosed pledging; the main limitation is that the filing does not establish how much insider stock was bought with cash rather than received through compensation.

## management-governance / 05_board-and-shareholder-rights.md

# Board & Shareholder Rights — BURL

**Scope and timing.** This is a U.S./NYSE governance read. The most recent proxy in the frozen pool is dated April 2, 2026 and describes a 12-member board before the May 19 annual meeting; it says Mary Ann Tocio would not stand for re-election and the board would then fall to 11. No post-meeting Form 8-K or revised board roster is in the admitted pool, so the tables label this as the proxy-date snapshot rather than inventing a September composition. [FY2026 DEF 14A, pp. 3–4, 11]

## 1. Board Composition

| Feature | Detail | Source |
|---|---|---|
| Board size | 12 at the April 2 proxy date; the proxy states that Tocio's May 19 retirement would reduce it to 11. | [FY2026 DEF 14A, pp. 4, 11] |
| Independent directors (%) — vs the applicable floor | 11 of 12, or 91.7%, are NYSE-independent; O'Sullivan is the CEO and the sole non-independent director. This clears the U.S. listing floor and the module's ≥50% Green band. | [FY2026 DEF 14A, pp. 3–4, 24] |
| Chair / CEO split? Chair non-executive, non-promoter? Designated (not rotating)? | Split. John Mahoney has been independent chair since February 2020; Michael O'Sullivan is CEO. The board elects the chair annually, so the chair is designated but not permanent. | [FY2026 DEF 14A, pp. 21–22] |
| Woman director / independent woman director where mandated | Four of the 12 proxy-date directors are women—Goodman, Rodriguez, Sen and Tocio—and all are shown as independent. The post-retirement count is not evidenced in the admitted pool. | [FY2026 DEF 14A, p. 4] |
| Meeting count, longest gap, ID present in every quorum (A1-14) | Four board meetings in FY2025; every director attended at least 92% of board and assigned-committee meetings. The filing does not disclose the longest gap or an independent-director quorum for each meeting. | [FY2026 DEF 14A, p. 24] |
| Audit / Comp (NRC) / Nominating committee independence | Audit: five NYSE/SEC-independent members, all financial experts, eight meetings. Compensation: three independent members, four meetings. NCGC: four independent members, four meetings. | [FY2026 DEF 14A, pp. 24, 32–34] |

| Mary Ann Tocio* | Not disclosed | ID | 2015 | ~11 years | ≥92% | ≥1 known: BURL; no current external-board biography because not nominated | BURL: Compensation; NCGC chair | **Tenure >10 years**; retirement expressly not due to disagreement | Clean, coverage-limited | [FY2026 DEF 14A, pp. 4, 11, 24, 32–34; 07, Person Register] |

\*Tocio was a proxy-date director but was due to retire at the May 19, 2026 annual meeting.

The legal-independence statistic is favorable, but the true-independence test is not. Five of 11 legally independent directors at the proxy date—Hitch, Mahoney, McNamara, Sullivan and Tocio—had served more than 10 years. Tocio's planned departure would reduce that count to four, still above the Registry's Red threshold of two. This is a tenure signal, not evidence that any director has a conflicted transaction or failed the `07` integrity screen. [FY2026 DEF 14A, pp. 4, 11–17, 24; 07, Person Register]

## 3. Related-Party Transactions & Group Leakage

No material related-party transactions were disclosed under Item 404 from the start of FY2025 through the proxy date. The proxy says directors and officers provide information for the related-party review and that the Audit Committee approves transactions subject to the policy. This is evidence of approval design and of no Item 404-reportable transaction; it is not a substitute for `09`'s item-level A5 quantification or proof that no transaction exists under every accounting definition. [FY2026 DEF 14A, p. 74]

|---|---:|---:|---:|---:|---|---|

| Loans / advances / guarantees to related parties | Not disclosed; no Item 404-reportable transaction | Not calculable | Not calculable | Not calculable | Not assessable from amounts | [FY2026 DEF 14A, p. 74] |
| Royalty / brand / management fees to promoter group | Not applicable: no promoter group identified; no Item 404-reportable transaction | Not calculable | Not calculable | Not calculable | Not assessable from amounts | [FY2026 DEF 14A, p. 74] |
| Rent / other payments to promoter entities | Not applicable: no promoter group identified; no Item 404-reportable transaction | Not calculable | Not calculable | Not calculable | Not assessable from amounts | [FY2026 DEF 14A, p. 74] |
| Outstanding receivables from related parties | Not disclosed; no Item 404-reportable transaction | Not calculable | Not calculable | Not calculable | Not assessable from amounts | [FY2026 DEF 14A, p. 74] |
| Cash / deposits placed with a related financial entity | No related financial counterparty identified in the proxy disclosure | Not calculable | Not calculable | Not calculable | Not applicable on available evidence | [FY2026 DEF 14A, p. 74] |
| Borrowings drawn from a related financial entity (and the approved cap) | No related financial counterparty identified in the proxy disclosure | Not calculable | Not calculable | Not calculable | Not applicable on available evidence | [FY2026 DEF 14A, p. 74] |

| Ratio quoted | Numerator + its basis | Denominator + its basis | Matched-basis version | Basis mismatch? |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| RPT / revenue | No disclosed cumulative RPT numerator | No relevant denominator required because numerator is absent | Not calculable | No ratio quoted |
| RPT / PAT | No disclosed cumulative RPT numerator | No relevant denominator required because numerator is absent | Not calculable | No ratio quoted |
| RPT / net worth | No disclosed period-end RPT balance | No relevant denominator required because numerator is absent | Not calculable | No ratio quoted |
| Cash with related financial entity / cash | No related-financial-entity exposure disclosed | No relevant denominator required because numerator is absent | Not calculable | No ratio quoted |

The CIQ supplier relationship graph names several Burlington group entities and has a recent-disclosures-only scope. It does not provide an Item 404 related-person transaction, transaction amount, approved cap, or audit-committee review record; group affiliation must not be misread as arm's-length RPT evidence. No related financial counterparty needing an equity-interest, regulator, cap, utilisation or auditor-review assessment was identified. [relationships.json, scope notes and group-affiliation fields]

## 4. Takeover Defenses & Voting Rights

| Feature | Present? | Detail | Source |
|---|---|---|---|
| Poison pill | No | The proxy says there is no stockholder rights plan or poison pill. | [FY2026 DEF 14A, p. 3] |
| Classified / staggered board | Yes, temporary | The phased declassification completes by the 2027 annual meeting. Until then, the charter retains staggered classes. | [FY2026 DEF 14A, p. 11; FY2025 Form 10-K, Item 1A, p. 17] |
| Dual-class / unequal voting | No | One vote per common share; no cumulative voting; proxy also states no unequal voting rights. | [FY2026 DEF 14A, pp. 3, 7] |
| Majority vs plurality director voting | Majority in uncontested elections | A nominee must receive more votes for than against, and an unsuccessful incumbent must tender a resignation; the board must decide within 90 days. | [FY2026 DEF 14A, p. 21] |
| Shareholder ability to call meetings / act by written consent | No / No | The 10-K says stockholders may not call special meetings and may not act by written consent. Proxy access is available to a group of up to 20 holders with ≥3% held continuously for three years, for the greater of two nominees or 20% of the board. | [FY2025 Form 10-K, Item 1A, p. 17; FY2026 DEF 14A, p. 21] |

## 5. Minority-Shareholder Protection Read

**Adequate.** The board has a 91.7% legal-independence ratio, an independent chair, fully independent standing committees, majority voting in uncontested elections and proxy access. [FY2026 DEF 14A, pp. 3–4, 21, 24, 32–34]

The main counterweight is true independence: five proxy-date independent directors had served more than 10 years, meeting the Registry's Red threshold even though none was given a Material or Disqualifying `07` grade. Minority holders also cannot call a special meeting or act by written consent, and the phased declassification and 75% removal hurdle remain until the 2027 annual meeting. [FY2026 DEF 14A, pp. 11, 21, 24; FY2025 Form 10-K, Item 1A, p. 17; 07, Person Register]

## management-governance / 06_candor-and-disclosure-quality.md

# Candor & Disclosure Quality — BURL

## 1. Owning the Misses

The completed promise-versus-delivery tests supplied by `01_management-and-track-record` contain no management miss: FY2025 Adjusted EBIT exceeded its target, the FY2023–FY2025 PSU Adjusted EPS targets cleared their maximum payout, and Q2 FY2026 was within or above the Q1 guide. The current Q3 FY2026 EPS outlook is lower than last year's $1.80, but it is forward guidance, not an actual miss; management attributes the planned decline to the quantified reinvestment of tariff refunds. [S01: Management & Track Record, §4] [S03: Q2 FY2026 earnings release, Outlook]

| Miss (from 01) | How It Was Communicated | Owned or Obscured? | Source |
|---|---|---|---|
| No completed management miss identified in the supplied delivery record | Not applicable. The closest current adverse comparison is Q3 FY2026 Adjusted EPS guidance of $1.60–$1.70 versus $1.80 a year earlier; the release explicitly attributes the margin and EPS effect to the planned reinvestment of approximately 40% of the $55m tariff refund in Q3 and 60% in Q4. | Not assessable as an owning-the-miss test; this is a disclosed forecast choice, not a delivered miss. | [S01: Management & Track Record, §4] [S03: Q2 FY2026 earnings release, Outlook] |

## 2. Non-GAAP / Adjustment Aggressiveness

| Signal | Detail | Source |
|---|---|---|
| Recurring "one-off" addbacks | FY2025 adjusted earnings excluded net favourable lease costs of $7.7m, impairment of $9.9m and litigation of $4.2m. Each category also appeared in FY2023 and FY2024, so these should not be described as wholly one-off. | [S02: FY2025 Form 10-K, Results of Operations—non-GAAP reconciliation] [S07: Earnings Quality, §§4–5] |
| GAAP-to-adjusted gap (magnitude / trend) | FY2025 GAAP net income was $610.2m and Adjusted Net Income was $625.7m: a $15.5m, 2.5% gap. Q2 FY2026 is different in direction: Adjusted EPS of $2.37 explicitly removes the $0.64-per-share tariff-refund benefit as well as lease costs, while GAAP EPS was $2.88. | [S02: FY2025 Form 10-K, Results of Operations—non-GAAP reconciliation] [S03: Q2 FY2026 earnings release, pp. 1–2 and Reconciliation of Non-GAAP Financial Measures] [S07: Earnings Quality, §7] |
| Does adjusted framing flatter reality? | Partly. The recurring exclusions make adjusted results less conservative than GAAP in FY2025, but the magnitude is modest and the Q2 presentation removes, rather than keeps, the large $55m positive tariff refund. The company provides historical reconciliations, while forward Adjusted EBIT/EPS have no quantitative GAAP reconciliation because it says the required estimates are inherently difficult to predict. | [S02: FY2025 Form 10-K, Results of Operations—non-GAAP reconciliation] [S03: Q2 FY2026 earnings release, Outlook and Note Regarding Non-GAAP Financial Measures] |

## 3. Disclosure Transparency

Burlington reports one consolidated operating segment, so it cannot provide separate segment economics; its FY2025 10-K nevertheless identifies net income, Adjusted Net Income, Adjusted EBITDA, Adjusted EBIT, comparable-store sales, gross margin, inventory and liquidity as key measures, and the Q2 call gave the tariff-refund amount, reinvestment timing, inventory mix, store count and operating drivers. [S02: FY2025 Form 10-K, Note 2—Segment Information; Results of Operations] [S05: Q2 FY2026 earnings call, prepared remarks]

The same principal KPI set is visible across FY2022–FY2025 annual disclosures and the Q1/Q2 FY2026 calls, and no KPI was identified as redefined or dropped after deterioration. The evidence does not demonstrate the five full years needed for the A7-03 Green band, and category- or region-level profitability is not disclosed; the latter is a limitation of a one-segment issuer, not evidence of a transparency reduction. [S06: FY2024 Annual Report, MD&A—Key performance and non-GAAP measures] [S02: FY2025 Form 10-K, Results of Operations] [S04: Q1 FY2026 earnings call, prepared remarks] [S05: Q2 FY2026 earnings call, prepared remarks and Q&A]

## 4. Tone in Bad Times

The two supplied verbatim calls cover quarters that beat guidance; the closest recent stress test is the Q3 FY2026 guide, where management set out the lower $1.60–$1.70 Adjusted EPS range, the 60–80bp operating-margin decline, the 40%/60% tariff-refund reinvestment split, and the weather and consumer risks rather than calling the impact immaterial. [S03: Q2 FY2026 earnings release, Outlook] [S05: Q2 FY2026 earnings call, prepared remarks]

In Q&A, management answered directly on tariff-refund uncertainty, cannibalisation, weather exposure, consumer pressure and the trade-off between sales and margin. The language is promotional in a good quarter (for example, calling underlying momentum “extremely robust”), but the numerical disclosures and direct risk answers mean no evasiveness was identified; a real delivered bad-quarter transcript is not in the admitted record. [S04: Q1 FY2026 earnings call, Q&A] [S05: Q2 FY2026 earnings call, Q&A]

## 5. Candor Read

**Mixed.** Management was specific about the $55m tariff refund, removed it from its underlying-Q2 presentation, and explained why it lowered Q3 margin and EPS while keeping the full-year effect neutral. The counterweight is recurring exclusions labelled non-GAAP adjustments and no actual management miss or bad-result call in the available sample, so the evidence does not support a candid / high-trust conclusion.

The single biggest candor signal is the explicit treatment of the tariff refund: $55m received in Q2, about 40% to be reinvested in Q3 and 60% in Q4, with a stated neutral full-year impact. The recurring lease-cost, impairment and litigation exclusions are a medium-severity caution, not evidence that the Q2 result was concealed.

RF-DISC-002 (recurring "one-off" / aggressive non-GAAP add-backs)

Registry directorship history, primary court dockets, comprehensive sanctions/PEP databases, credential licences and full adverse-media archives were not available, so all are **Clean with coverage limitations**, not fully cleared. The row-level decisive fact and coverage for each appear in Section 1; no person had an entity exposure, an attributed adverse hit, a material buzz item, or a red-flag ID.

The latter five are not treated as current group entities absent primary confirmation. Each is named in the machine network handoff with `not_run` or reduced-depth scope, rather than being silently treated as clean. ## 3B. Lineage & Phoenix Read (A17-02, A17-03)

The documents support a disclosed continuity story: first BCF store in 1972, BCFWC New Jersey organization/reincorporation, Bain's 2006 acquisition, IPO in 2013, and later Burlington branding. [BURL Investors, “Our History”, accessed 2026-09-29; BURL 2015 DEF 14A, corporate history] No filing or public source in the admitted evidence shows that an entity failed, that assets were shifted from an insolvent predecessor, or that a creditor-avoidance phoenix occurred. The business, brands and address continuity are therefore explained by disclosed group history, though the predecessor/former-name legal sweep belongs to `12` and remains pending that handoff.

The 40-full-dossier ceiling did not bind: four Tier-A dossiers were expanded and 36 completed Tier-B/C compact dossiers are in Section 1. The entity overflow is declared above, not silently dropped.

## 6. Provenance & Next Data Request

The filing supplied 43 subjects and the discovery work added four (the former legal name, BCFWC lineage, BCF Direct and founder Milstein); no subject was user supplied. Independent directors are excluded from beneficial-interest analysis only; their individual integrity coverage remains in Section 1. The unresolved aggregator conflict is `Burlington Coat Factory Investment Holdings, Inc.` (Exhibit 21) versus CIQ's similar plural spelling/former-name claim. **Single highest-value next data request:** an identifier-linked paid U.S. people-and-entity registry/court/sanctions batch for the 40-person roster and BCFWC/BCF Direct, including historical officer roles and state case dockets.

## 7. People & Network Integrity Read

No person or scoped entity has an attributed Material or Disqualifying fact in the admitted evidence. The only decision-relevant network fact is a disclosed historic BCFWC lineage that explains the company’s 1972 history and trademark ownership; it is not a phoenix read. The riskiest unresolved point is not a red flag but coverage: the roster lacks unique officer IDs and the complete registry/court/global-list sweep could not run. A rational minority holder should treat the current integrity read as **PASS, coverage-limited**, with the People & Network Integrity Score capped at 65 until the missing primary screens and the `12` lineage handoff are complete.

## management-governance / 08_audit-and-assurance-quality.md

_Source: `08_audit-and-assurance-quality.md`_

# Audit & Assurance Quality — BURL

## 1. The Assurance Stack

| Role | Firm / Person | Network | Signing partner | Appointed | Tenure (yrs) | Local cap | Source |
|---|---|---|---|---|---:|---|---|
| Statutory auditor | Deloitte & Touche LLP | Deloitte | Not named in the reviewed BURL filings or frozen-pool Form AP material | 1983 | 43 | U.S.: no mandatory firm-rotation cap; BURL says Deloitte rotates the lead partner every five years | [FY2025 Form 10-K, Report of Independent Registered Public Accounting Firm, p. 41]; [FY2026 DEF 14A, Proposal 2, p. 37] |
| Principal component auditors | Not disclosed | Not disclosed | Not disclosed | Not disclosed | n/a | U.S. component-auditor disclosure is not provided in the reviewed report | [FY2025 Form 10-K, Report of Independent Registered Public Accounting Firm, p. 41; Exhibit 21.1] |
| Internal auditor | Individual and firm not named; head of internal audit reports to the Audit Committee | Not disclosed | n/a | Not disclosed | n/a | No U.S. statutory appointment cap identified | [FY2026 DEF 14A, Risk Oversight, p. 23] |
| Secretarial auditor (India) | Not Applicable — U.S. SEC issuer | n/a | n/a | n/a | n/a | MR-3 does not apply | [FY2026 DEF 14A, Cover] |

Paul J. Sullivan, a retired PwC partner, chairs the Audit Committee; that is governance context, not a substitute for a named Deloitte signing partner. The current signing partner could not be identified from the evidence snapshot. An official PCAOB Form AP search surfaced Susan Marie O'Mara for the FY2022 BURL audit only; it cannot be assumed that she signed the FY2025 audit after the disclosed five-year rotation policy. [FY2026 DEF 14A, Board Committees, p. 33; PCAOB Form AP for BURL audit dated 2023-03-13, queried 2026-09-29]

## 2. Opinions, Controls & Restatements (A4-05, A4-09, A7-02)

| FY | Opinion (standalone) | Opinion (consolidated) | EoM / going concern | IFC/ICFR opinion | Restatement / prior-period adjustment | Verdict | Source |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| FY2025 (ended 2026-01-31) | Not Applicable — BURL files consolidated U.S. statements, not separate standalone accounts | Unqualified; Deloitte's report covers FY2025, FY2024 and FY2023 results | No qualification, emphasis-of-matter or going-concern paragraph identified in the report | Unqualified ICFR opinion | FY2025 restatement-recovery indicator unchecked; no restatement found in the reviewed filing | Green for opinion and ICFR; five-year restatement history remains incomplete | [FY2025 Form 10-K, Report of Independent Registered Public Accounting Firm, p. 41; Report on ICFR, Item 9A; Cover] |
| FY2024 (ended 2025-02-01) | Not Applicable — U.S. consolidated-only filing | Unqualified; Deloitte report covers FY2024, FY2023 and FY2022 results | No qualification, emphasis-of-matter or going-concern paragraph identified in the report | Unqualified ICFR opinion | FY2024 restatement-recovery indicator unchecked; no restatement found in the reviewed filing | Green for opinion and ICFR; five-year restatement history remains incomplete | [FY2024 Annual Report, Report of Independent Registered Public Accounting Firm, pp. 48–49; Report on ICFR, pp. 91–92; Cover] |
| FY2023 (ended 2024-02-03) | Not Applicable — U.S. consolidated-only filing | Included in Deloitte's FY2025 three-year audit opinion | No qualification, emphasis-of-matter or going-concern paragraph identified in the FY2025 three-year report | No FY2023 year-end ICFR report was supplied separately | No FY2023 restatement check was supplied separately | Insufficient Data on standalone ICFR/restatement lookback; audited consolidated opinion clean | [FY2025 Form 10-K, Report of Independent Registered Public Accounting Firm, p. 41] |

No hard-lock deference applies: the upstream scan states, verbatim, “No disqualifier triggered.” It specifically records no audit qualification, going-concern note or material restatement. This report documents the assurance layer and does not re-decide that lock. [business-model/01_disqualifier-scan, Triggered Disqualifiers — Detail]

## 3. Calibre, Rotation & Regulator Discipline (A4-01, A4-02, A4-13)

| Test | Raw value | Green band | Red band | Verdict | Source |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| A4-01 — tenure vs local cap; rotation real? | Deloitte has audited BURL since 1983 (43 years). BURL says Deloitte rotates the lead engagement partner every five years and the Audit Committee interviews/selects the candidate; actual partner names and rotation dates are not disclosed. | U.S. lead-partner rotation is orderly and real | Same-network affiliate swap, or cap dodged via loopholes | Amber — no U.S. firm cap is breached, but 43-year firm tenure makes the undisclosed implementation of partner rotation material to independence. There is no outgoing/incoming firm because no firm change occurred. | [FY2025 Form 10-K, Report of Independent Registered Public Accounting Firm, p. 41]; [FY2026 DEF 14A, Proposal 2, p. 37] |
| A4-02 — firm calibre vs company complexity | Deloitte is a Big Four PCAOB-registered annual firm. The PCAOB's firm record lists Deloitte disciplinary proceedings in 2007, 2013 and 2018; the 2018 order censured Deloitte and imposed a $500,000 penalty over unrelated Jack Henry audits. | Big-4 / peer-reviewed national firm, listed practice sized to this company | Firm too small for complexity, or firm/partner with disciplinary history | Red — capacity is not in doubt, but the strict disciplinary-history band is met. The order concerns another issuer and does not establish a BURL audit error. | [PCAOB Firm Summary for Deloitte & Touche LLP, queried 2026-09-29]; [PCAOB Release 105-2018-008, pp. 1–3] |
| A4-13 — PCAOB / SEC orders vs firm or signing partner | Deloitte is currently PCAOB-registered, but the PCAOB record lists firm proceedings in 2007, 2013 and 2018. The current BURL signing partner is not identified, so a partner-specific sweep could not run. | No orders against firm or signing partner | Active order or debarment (also RF-AUD-007) | Red — historical final firm orders mean “no orders” cannot be claimed. Current PCAOB registration is evidence against a present firm debarment, not evidence that historical orders did not occur. Partner coverage is limited. | [PCAOB Firm Summary for Deloitte & Touche LLP, queried 2026-09-29]; [PCAOB Release 105-2018-008, pp. 1–3]; [PCAOB Release 105-2013-008, 2013-10-22] |

The PCAOB 2018 order found that Deloitte's work on an unrelated issuer's FY2012–FY2014 audits did not obtain sufficient appropriate evidence to support opinions and led to a $500,000 penalty. The PCAOB firm register nevertheless shows Deloitte as currently registered. This is a firm-quality and diligence finding, not a claim that BURL's financial statements are misstated. [PCAOB Release 105-2018-008, pp. 1–3; PCAOB Firm Summary for Deloitte & Touche LLP, queried 2026-09-29]

## 4. Economic Independence — Fees (A4-06, A4-07)

| FY | Audit fee | Non-audit fee (incl. network firms) | Non-audit / audit (%) | Prohibited services? | Fee vs peer median | Revenue growth | Verdict | Source |
|---|---:|---:|---:|---|---|---:|---|---|
| FY2025 | $1,733,625 | $98,544 = $25,000 audit-related + $73,544 tax + $0 all-other; network-firm inclusion is not separately disclosed | 5.7% | No SOX §201-prohibited service identified in the disclosed categories; audit-related work was an S-8 filing, tax work was returns/advisory | Not assessable — no matched peer fee table supplied | 8.8% total-revenue growth; audit fee rose 2.1% | Amber — disclosed ratio is below 25% and fee did not fall, but network fees and peer-median comparison are not evidenced. | [FY2026 DEF 14A, Principal Accountant Fees and Services, p. 38]; [FY2025 Form 10-K, Consolidated Statements of Income, p. 43] |
| FY2024 | $1,697,720 | $181,851 = $0 audit-related + $36,729 tax + $145,122 all-other; network-firm inclusion is not separately disclosed | 10.7% | No SOX §201-prohibited service identified; all-other was climate-data assurance readiness | Not assessable — no matched peer fee table supplied | Prior-year comparator only | Amber — disclosed ratio is below 25%; network fees and peer-median comparison are not evidenced. | [FY2026 DEF 14A, Principal Accountant Fees and Services, p. 38] |

All disclosed FY2025 and FY2024 services were pre-approved by the committee or its chair under delegated authority, with at-least-quarterly review. That is a control, not proof that no network firm billed BURL, because the disclosure does not provide a network-firm split. [FY2026 DEF 14A, Policy on Audit Committee Pre-Approval of Audit and Permissible Non-Audit Services, p. 38]
