| Date | Who left (auditor / CFO / CS / internal auditor) | Mid-term? | Stated reason | Quarter's limited review completed? (SEBI Oct-2019) | Days to results / fundraise | Successor named? | Source |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| FY2022–FY2026 audit history | No Deloitte departure identified; Capital IQ's auditor export lists Deloitte for each fiscal year | No | Not applicable — no change disclosed | Not Applicable — U.S. issuer, not SEBI | n/a | n/a | [Capital IQ Auditors — Auditors, FY2022–FY2026 — vendor export]; [FY2025 Form 10-K, Report of Independent Registered Public Accounting Firm, p. 41] |
| 2026-03-19 and 2026-08-27 | Kristin Wolfe signed as CFO on the FY2025 10-K and Q2 FY2026 10-Q | No departure identified | n/a | Not Applicable — U.S. issuer, not SEBI | n/a | Incumbent CFO | [FY2025 Form 10-K, Signatures]; [Q2 FY2026 Form 10-Q, Certifications] |
| Reviewed period | Corporate Secretary Karen Leu is current; internal-audit leader is not named; a complete 8-K/current-report archive was not supplied | Not assessable | Not assessable | Not Applicable — U.S. issuer, not SEBI | Not assessable | Not assessable | [FY2026 DEF 14A, Notice and Governance sections; Governance Data Triage, Most Recent Sources] |

There is no auditor resignation and no CFO-plus-auditor exit cluster identified in the reviewed evidence. The full three-year gatekeeper timeline cannot be certified: no complete 8-K archive or named internal-audit leader was supplied. A4-15 is therefore Insufficient Data, not Green.

## 9. Audit Committee ↔ Auditor Engagement (A4-12)

| Signal | Evidence | Verdict | Source |
|---|---|---|---|
| Private sessions (committee meets auditor without management) | Deloitte representatives, the head of internal audit, CEO and CFO regularly meet in private sessions with the Audit Committee. | Green | [FY2026 DEF 14A, Risk Oversight, p. 23] |
| Auditor attendance at committee meetings | Deloitte reports directly to the Audit Committee; the committee reviewed audited statements, PCAOB/SEC-required matters and Deloitte's independence letter. | Green | [FY2026 DEF 14A, Proposal 2, p. 37; Audit Committee Report, p. 39] |

## 10. Read

BURL's assurance is **mixed — watch the named link**. Deloitte issued clean consolidated opinions for the last three reported years and an unqualified FY2025 ICFR opinion, while disclosed non-audit fees were only 5.7% of audit fees in FY2025. The weakest link is not a modified BURL opinion; it is the combination of a 43-year audit-firm tenure, historical PCAOB discipline of Deloitte, no current signing-partner identification, and no disclosed component-auditor coverage. Trust would increase with the current BURL Form AP/partner record, a partner-specific discipline sweep, and an Audit Committee disclosure of Deloitte's principal/component coverage plus all network-firm fees.

# Related-Party & Group Forensics — BURL

## 1. RPT Intensity & the Applicable Regime (A5-01)

BURL is a U.S. issuer. The applicable screen for each fiscal year is SEC Regulation S-K Item 404(a): a transaction exceeding $120,000 in which a related person has a direct or indirect material interest must be disclosed. This is a disclosure threshold, not a measure of every de-minimis consolidated intercompany transaction. The current proxy covers the period from the beginning of FY2025 through April 2, 2026; the frozen pool does not contain the corresponding FY2023 or FY2024 proxy disclosures. [FY2026 Proxy Statement, Certain Relationships and Related Person Transactions]

| FY | Aggregate RPTs ex-dividends | % of revenue | % of PAT | % of assets | Material-RPT line that year | Above the line? | Minority-approved? | Verdict | Source |
|---|---:|---:|---:|---:|---|---|---|---|---|
| FY2023 | Not assessable — no contemporaneous Item 404 disclosure in the frozen pool | N/A | N/A | N/A | Item 404(a): $120k | Not assessable | Not assessable | Insufficient Data | [FY2024 Form 10-K, Schedule I; frozen-pool inventory, 2026-09-29] |
| FY2024 | Not assessable — no contemporaneous Item 404 disclosure in the frozen pool | N/A | N/A | N/A | Item 404(a): $120k | Not assessable | Not assessable | Insufficient Data | [FY2024 Form 10-K, Schedule I; frozen-pool inventory, 2026-09-29] |
| FY2025 (ended 2026-01-31) | $0 of Item-404-reportable related-person transactions; the proxy does not quantify every non-reportable consolidated intercompany flow | 0.0% of $11,549.6m | 0.0% of $610.2m | 0.0% of $9,919.1m | Item 404(a): $120k | No reported transaction above the line | Not required on the disclosed $0 category | Amber — current reportable screen clear; full aggregate RPT total is not disclosed | [FY2026 Proxy Statement, Certain Relationships and Related Person Transactions; FY2025 Form 10-K, Consolidated Statements of Income and Balance Sheets; earnings/01, Annual Financial Table] |

The disclosed Item 404 category is $0 / $11,549.6m revenue = 0.0% and $0 / $10,700.7m operating-cost/expense base = 0.0%. It is below the 25% test, but that hard-lock conclusion belongs to `business-model/01_disqualifier-scan`; this report does not re-adjudicate it. The result does not prove that de-minimis or consolidated intercompany transactions were zero. [business-model/01_disqualifier-scan, Disqualifier 3; FY2026 Proxy Statement, Certain Relationships and Related Person Transactions]

## 2. The RPT Ledger (every named counterparty)

The proxy names no Item-404-reportable related-person counterparty. The only named group financing channel found in the filed parent-only schedules is the joint BCFWC/Burlington Merchandising obligation below; it is eliminated in consolidation and is not a payment to a promoter or a separate controller.

| Counterparty | Relationship | Type (sales/purchases/royalty/rent/loan/guarantee/other) | FY2023 | FY2024 | FY2025 (latest) | Trend | Arm's-length basis disclosed? | Source |
|---|---|---|---:|---:|---:|---|---|---|
| Burlington Coat Factory Warehouse Corporation (BCFWC) and Burlington Merchandising Corporation (BMC), jointly and severally | Wholly owned subsidiaries | Parent intercompany note receivable supporting the Parent's convertible notes | $453.2m joint amount | $453.2m joint amount | $297.1m joint amount | Down $156.1m (34.4%) from FY2024; do not add the joint amount twice | Terms and interest rate stated as consistent with the Parent's 2025/2027 convertible notes; no separate third-party pricing study disclosed | [FY2024 Form 10-K, Schedule I, Note 4; FY2025 Form 10-K, Schedule I, Note 4] |

There is no disclosed external promoter, executive, director, or >5% holder on the other side of this joint note. The history is therefore a disclosed intra-group funding flow, not evidence that value moved to a controller. Its size still warrants an Amber, rather than a Green, group-topology read. [FY2025 Form 10-K, Schedule I, Note 4; FY2026 Proxy Statement, Certain Relationships and Related Person Transactions]

No both-role lead exists in the disclosed RPT data, so there is no circularity evidence and no round-tripping red flag. This is not a clearance of ordinary suppliers, which are outside Item 404 unless a related-person link exists.

**Matched-basis rule (CLAUDE.md §15).** All amounts below use the same January 31, 2026 period-end basis unless labelled as a fiscal-year flow. No maximum daily balance or approved-cap figure is divided by a period-end balance.

| Question | Raw value | Basis (both sides) | Matched ratio | Verdict | Source |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| Where does consolidated cash sit (parent vs subs)? | Parent cash $0.290m; consolidated cash $1,232.525m; implied subsidiary aggregate $1,232.235m | Parent-only and consolidated balance sheets, both 2026-01-31 period-end | Parent = 0.024% of consolidated cash; implied subs = 99.976% | Amber — cash legally sits in subsidiaries, but the filing shows $409.0m of FY2025 net contributions to Parent; entity-level cash is not disclosed | [FY2025 Form 10-K, Schedule I—Parent Balance Sheet and Cash Flows; Consolidated Balance Sheets] |
| Share held with a related finance company / treasury vehicle | No such vehicle disclosed | N/A | Not assessable | Not Applicable (no data) | [FY2025 Form 10-K, Exhibit 21.1; Schedule I] |
| Parent borrowing while subs hold the cash? | Parent long-term debt $294.691m against $0.290m parent cash; Parent received $408.971m net subsidiary contributions in FY2025 | Debt/cash are 2026-01-31 period-end; contributions are FY2025 annual flow and are not used as a ratio | 1,016x parent debt/cash at period-end; no mismatched flow/stock ratio is headlined | Amber — legal-parent liquidity depends on subsidiaries, but upstream contributions are disclosed and no opaque cash pocket is identified | [FY2025 Form 10-K, Schedule I—Parent Balance Sheet, Cash Flows and Note 1] |
| ICDs to group entities — amount, rate, tenor | $297.1m joint note receivable from BCFWC/BMC; terms match 2025/2027 convertible notes | 2026-01-31 period-end balance; terms per filed note | N/A | Amber — material intercompany exposure, down from $453.2m | [FY2025 Form 10-K, Schedule I, Note 4] |
| Evergreen / rolled-over ICDs? | $453.2m at 2024-02-03 and 2025-02-01; $297.1m at 2026-01-31 | Three year-end balances | Down 34.4% in FY2025 | Green on observed direction; term extension/rollover detail remains limited | [FY2024 Form 10-K, Schedule I, Note 4; FY2025 Form 10-K, Schedule I, Note 4] |

There is no related financial counterparty, equity interest, regulatory-supervision or approved-cap issue to record. The important limitation is different: the consolidated accounts show total cash, not legal-entity cash by subsidiary, so the location and fungibility of the $1.232bn subsidiary aggregate cannot be fully tested.

## 14. Read

No currently reportable value transfer to a promoter, executive, director, or >5% holder was disclosed: the FY2025 Item 404 category is $0, or 0.0% of reported revenue and PAT. The largest named group channel is the $297.1m BCFWC/BMC joint intercompany note, down from $453.2m, with terms matching the Parent's convertible notes; it is a material internal funding flow, not proven minority leakage. The structure has 25 named subsidiaries and almost all reported cash sits below the Parent, but disclosed $409.0m FY2025 upstream contributions argue against a demonstrated trapped-cash loop; no entity-level cash map or ownership tree is supplied. The single highest-value disclosure would be a legal-entity chart with cash, debt, guarantees, and intercompany balances by material subsidiary, plus prior-year Item 404 transaction schedules.

## management-governance / 10_contingent-liabilities-and-commitments.md

_Source: `10_contingent-liabilities-and-commitments.md`_

# Contingent Liabilities & Commitments — BURL

**Scope and evidence limit.** Burlington Stores, Inc. is a U.S. GAAP reporter with a January fiscal year-end; amounts are USD millions unless stated otherwise. The frozen pool supplies three comparable fiscal-year observations for letters of credit (FY2023–FY2025) and a current Q2 FY2026 update, rather than five annual reports. `balance-sheet-survival/05_off-balance-sheet-and-contingencies.md` is not present inside this continuation root, so this report proceeds from the frozen filing pool and does not recompute a survival-module result. No web/database posture sweep was run: the filings do not name or quantify individual disputes that could be searched without guessing, so all litigation-posture statements below are filings-only.

## 1. Headline Exposure (A7a-01)

| Measure | Amount | Denominator (figure + source) | % | Band (Green <5% NW / Red >25% NW or >50% PAT) | Verdict | Source |
|---|---:|---|---:|---|---|---|
| Quantified contingent liability — letters of credit arrangements | $58.0 | Net worth = $2,002.248 [Q2 FY2026 Form 10-Q, Condensed Consolidated Balance Sheets, p.5] | 2.90% | Green on net-worth test | Green | [Q2 FY2026 Form 10-Q, Note 11 — Commitments and Contingencies, p.20] |
| Quantified contingent liability — letters of credit arrangements | $58.0 | FY2025 PAT / net income = $610.153 [FY2025 Form 10-K, Consolidated Statements of Income, p.43] | 9.51% | Green on PAT test (<50%) | Green | [Q2 FY2026 Form 10-Q, Note 11 — Commitments and Contingencies, p.20] |
| Total commitments (separate line): inventory purchases for goods not received | $2,558.5 | Net worth = $2,002.248 [Q2 FY2026 Form 10-Q, Condensed Consolidated Balance Sheets, p.5] | 127.78% | Not a contingent-liability band; must not be added to the $58.0m CL total | Amber | [Q2 FY2026 Form 10-Q, Note 11 — Purchase Commitments, p.20] |

The $58.0m figure is the only quantified current contingent obligation in the note. It is not a complete estimate of all legal exposure: the filing describes litigation and regulatory proceedings but provides neither a monetary range nor a provision by matter. The $2,558.5m merchandise-purchase commitment is contractually important and larger than equity, but it would deliver inventory rather than represent a stand-alone contingent loss; it is therefore shown separately, not combined with CLs.

## 2. Item-by-Item Register & Movement (A7a-02, A7a-13)

| Item (as named in the note) | FY-3 | FY-2 | FY-1 | FY0 | Additions / Reversals / Crystallized | Posture change (forum, last outcome) | Explained in narrative? | Verdict | Source |
|---|---:|---:|---:|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| Letters of credit arrangements | $75.8 at Feb. 3, 2024 | $52.5 at Feb. 1, 2025 | $50.4 at Jan. 31, 2026 | $58.0 at Aug. 1, 2026 | Annual amounts declined 30.7% then 4.0%; Q2 amount rose 15.1% versus year-end. No call, reversal, or crystallization is disclosed. | Not a dispute; supports leases, insurance, utilities and merchandise arrangements. | Purpose and capacity disclosed; reason for the Q2 increase is not stated. | Amber — annual direction is down, but the current increase is unexplained. | [FY2024 Form 10-K, Note 14, pp.80–81]; [FY2025 Form 10-K, Note 14, p.70]; [Q2 FY2026 Form 10-Q, Note 11, p.20] |
| Gross unrecognized tax benefits (recognized tax liability, not a CL) | $3.132 at Feb. 3, 2024 | $2.349 at Feb. 1, 2025 | $1.566 at Jan. 31, 2026 | No Q2 update disclosed | $0.783m reduction for prior-year positions in each of FY2024 and FY2025; no additions or settlements shown in the annual roll-forward. | Ongoing state audits in several jurisdictions; appellate forum and individual outcomes not disclosed. | The tax-accounting roll-forward, interest and penalties, and audit window are disclosed. | Green — declining, accrued tax exposure; individual case posture cannot be tested. | [FY2025 Form 10-K, Note 12 — Income Taxes, gross unrecognized-tax-benefits reconciliation] |
| Inventory purchase commitments | Not disclosed in the FY2024 note for Feb. 3, 2024 | $1,594.0 at Feb. 1, 2025 | $1,774.0 at Jan. 31, 2026 | $2,558.5 at Aug. 1, 2026 | +11.3% across FY2024–FY2025 and +44.2% from year-end to Q2; the latter is not a like-for-like annual comparison. No cancellation, reversal, or loss information disclosed. | Not a dispute. | Amount disclosed, but cancellation terms and tie to a capex plan are not disclosed. | Amber — material operating commitment; it is not evidence of a crystallized loss. | [FY2024 Form 10-K, Note 14, p.81]; [FY2025 Form 10-K, Note 14, p.70]; [Q2 FY2026 Form 10-Q, Note 11, p.20] |
| Legal actions and regulatory proceedings | Amount not disclosed | Amount not disclosed | Amount not disclosed | Amount not disclosed | No item-level monetary movement can be constructed. | Class/collective wage-and-hour and California PAGA claims, plus commercial, product, employee, customer, IP, privacy and other matters; filings say matters are at various stages. | Narrative assertion that final resolution is not expected to be material; no matter-level range or outcome. | Insufficient Data — cannot test the flat-total trap or provisioning case by case. | [FY2024 Form 10-K, Item 3 and Note 14]; [FY2025 Form 10-K, Item 3 and Note 14]; [Q2 FY2026 Form 10-Q, Note 11, p.20] |

The item-level record does **not** show a hidden worsening inside a flat letter-of-credit total: annual LC arrangements fell from $75.8m to $50.4m before the current $58.0m increase. The legal bucket has no amount or docket-level posture, however, so it cannot be treated as clean merely because no total is printed. No silent disappearance or repeated crystallization was identified in the disclosed LC or tax-benefit roll-forwards; RF-CL-003 is not triggered from the available evidence.

## 11. Read

Burlington’s quantified current contingent obligation is $58.0m of letters of credit, only 2.90% of $2,002.248m of stockholders’ equity and 9.51% of FY2025 PAT of $610.153m. The largest disclosed contractual commitment is instead $2,558.5m of goods not yet received—127.78% of equity—which should be monitored as inventory-buying exposure, not folded into the CL total as though it were a loss. Tax provisions are explicitly recognized and the gross unrecognized-tax-benefit balance fell from $3.132m to $1.566m over two years; the real disclosure limit is that legal, regulatory, group-guarantee, recourse-financing and FX exposures are not quantified or separated. A rational minority holder should watch whether the current Q2 increase in LC arrangements and inventory commitments turns into higher working-capital strain, and request case-level amounts, provisions and outcomes if any legal matter becomes material.

### Hard Self-Check

- Every owned item A7a-01 through A7a-15 appears in the Universal Findings Table.
- Every percentage of net worth names the $2,002.248m Q2 FY2026 denominator and filing source; the PAT denominator is stated beside the relevant ratio.
- Movement is item-based; the unquantified legal bucket is identified as not testable rather than treated as a flat or clean total.
- No penalty demand is disclosed, so no tax penalty was discounted; no existing demand has been haircutted.
- All undisclosed categories are called “not separately disclosed,” not nil.
- No serial appeal loss is disclosed; RF-CL-002 and RF-CL-003 are therefore not triggered from available evidence.
- No balance-sheet-survival/05 input existed in the current continuation root, so no survival-module number was recomputed.
- No related-party guarantee is identified; RF-RPT-003 and RF-FIN-003 are not triggered on the measured $58.0m LC amount.
- No web posture claim is made, and no Sweep Log is required because no posture sweep ran.
- The risk-score table labels its higher-is-worse inversion.

## 2. Beneish M-Score (A8-14) — computed with python3, computation shown

The M-score is a statement-pattern screen, not proof of fraud. I computed the mandated formula from Section 1 using the Bash/Python method.

| Component | Value FY2025 | Non-manipulator mean | Manipulator zone | Verdict |
|---|---:|---:|---|---|
| DSRI (receivables outrunning sales) | 1.0989 | 1.031 | ≥1.465 | Green |
| GMI (gross margin deteriorating) | 0.9866 | 1.014 | ≥1.193 | Green |
| AQI (asset quality softening) | 0.9338 | 1.039 | ≥1.254 | Green |
| SGI (sales growth pressure) | 1.0879 | 1.134 | ≥1.607 | Green |
| DEPI (depreciation rate slowing) | 1.0965 | 1.001 | ≥1.077 | Amber — one zone component |
| SGAI (overhead vs sales) | 0.9893 | 1.054 | not banded | Green |
| LVGI (leverage rising) | 0.9882 | 1.037 | ≥1.111 | Green |
| TATA (accruals vs assets) | −0.0626 | 0.018 | ≥0.031 | Green |

Composite: M = −4.84 + 0.920×1.0989 + 0.528×0.9866 + 0.404×0.9338 + 0.892×1.0879 + 0.115×1.0965 − 0.172×0.9893 − 0.327×0.9882 + 4.679×(−0.0626) = **−2.6207**. [FY2025 Form 10-K, pp. 43, 45–46]; computation by this agent.

| Composite / rule | Value | Green band | Amber band | Red band | Verdict |
|---|---:|---|---|---|---|
| M-score | −2.6207 | < −2.22, no component in zone | −2.22 to −1.78 | > −1.78 | Amber: composite Green, but one zone component must be Amber. |
| Components in manipulator zone | 1 of 7 | 0 | 1–2 | ≥3 | Amber |

DEPI is the only zone signal: the depreciation rate fell from 12.79% to 11.67%. PP&E grew 33.5% and D&A rose 20.2%, so the filings do not establish an unexplained expense suppression. [FY2025 Form 10-K, pp. 45–46, 62]

## 3. Dechow F-Score & Accrual Battery (A8-17, A8-18) — computed with python3, computation shown

The Dechow F-score estimates misstatement probability; 1.00 is the average company. RSST is total accruals, or profit booked before cash, across working capital, non-current operating assets and financing. Soft assets are assets other than cash and PP&E, which rely more on estimates.

| Test | Value | Green band | Red band | Verdict | Evidence |
|---|---:|---|---|---|---|
| Dechow F-score | 0.8819 | <1.00 | ≥1.85; ≥2.45 high | Green | Required Python computation. |
| RSST accruals / average assets | 2.13% | ≤3% | ≥10% | Green | ΔWC −172.098 + ΔNCO 742.551 + ΔFIN −371.517 = 198.936; average assets 9,344.735. |
| Soft assets / total assets | 55.67% | <50% | >65% and rising | Amber | (9,919.057 − 3,164.218 − 1,232.525) / 9,919.057. |
| Δ receivables / average assets | 0.18% | <2% | ≥5% | Green | 17.217 / 9,344.735. |
| Δ inventory / average assets | 0.65% | <2% | ≥5% | Green | 61.128 / 9,344.735. |
| A8-18 — issuance in a flagged year | $500.0m Term B-7 incremental loans; F 0.8819 and TATA −6.26% | none while flagged | issued while F ≥1.85 or TATA ≥3.1% | Green | Debt was raised, but not in a battery-flagged year. |

Inputs and output: WC FY2023/FY2024/FY2025 = −613.418/−467.515/−639.613; NCO = 2,093.636/2,554.122/3,296.673; FIN = −1,408.645/−1,710.809/−2,082.326. Cash sales rose from 10,603.025 to 11,532.390, or 8.765%; ROA rose 6.113% to 6.529%. The Python output was pred = −5.7219, probability = 0.3263%, F = 0.8819. [FY2024 Form 10-K, pp. 50, 52–53]; [FY2025 Form 10-K, pp. 43, 45–46]

## 12. Forensics Read

BURL’s measured batteries do not show a current manipulation pattern: Beneish M = **−2.6207** and Dechow F = **0.8819**, both below Red cutoffs. The worst measured signal is DEPI of **1.0965**, which can rhyme with delayed depreciation, but PP&E grew 33.5% and D&A rose 20.2%, so the filings do not establish expense suppression. CFO/PAT of **2.02×**, CFO/EBITDA of **99.2%**, and collections growth of 8.77% corroborate earnings; cash taxes were nearly flat and require a timing check. Cash authenticity is **not proven from available data** because interest income is scope-mixed and the public audit report does not disclose bank-confirmation procedures. A minority holder has no measured books-manipulation red flag today, but should require FY2026 detail on cash interest, receivable ageing and capitalised-development additions.

# Regulatory, Legal & Compliance — BURL

**As of:** 2026-09-29. **Scope:** Burlington Stores, Inc. (NYSE: BURL; CIK 0001579298; Delaware). This is a coverage-limited company-level public-record sweep, not a finding that the record is clear. The independent databases available in this run did identify court and agency records; several complete issuer-search interfaces, the NYSE issuer-fine history, and full rating-action histories were unavailable. Accordingly, the Legal & Regulatory Risk Score is floored at 40/100 (inverted — higher is worse) and confidence is capped at 70.

## 9. Read

**Hygiene lapses, watch.** The public record adds a court-approved $5.2m wage settlement and an active, unquantified commercial case; the filings disclose both categories but not the case names or known settlement amount. The known settlement is below the module’s materiality threshold, so there is no demonstrated material undisclosed matter or regulator-enforcement cap. The anonymous hotline is real, but the lack of a named ombudsperson and complaint statistics means its effectiveness is not proven; incomplete regulator, exchange, sanctions, court and ratings coverage keeps risk at the 40 floor rather than calling the record clean.
